It’s pretty much everything. My mind just keeps jumping from one thing to another. I start stressing about whether my family will think I can’t handle things. Um, it's actually been going on for about a year and three months now. It started right around the time I began an evening graduate certificate program... while still keeping my full-time analyst job. So, yeah, it's been pretty much every day since then.\n\nDoctor: When you start worrying, do you find it hard to stop or control the worry?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's really hard to stop. I try to just tell myself to calm down and focus on one thing, but it doesn't work. One worry just immediately leads right into another one. Before I know it, I'm checking my calendar, looking over my budget, and going through my assignments over and over again... and I end up not actually getting anything done. It's like I just can't turn it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel physically tense, like your muscles are tight or you can’t relax?\n\nPatient: Um, I haven't really noticed that, actually. I mean, I feel stressed out all the time, but I don't think my muscles get super tight or anything. I don't think so, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed كثيراً lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm stressed and overwhelmed with everything I have to do, but I don't feel depressed or sad. Like, I'm not pacing around the room or anything. It's more just... my mind is always racing. I don't feel keyed up in my body, it's just my thoughts that won't stop.\n\nDoctor: Have these worries been interfering with how well you function in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes, they really have. I've actually started missing deadlines at work recently, which is... well, it's not like me. And I'm falling behind in my certificate classes, too. I just can't seem to keep up. I feel like my brain is so used to spinning out of control now that it might just latch onto something else. Um, it's actually been going on for about a year and three months now. It started right around the time I began an evening graduate certificate program... while still keeping my full-time analyst job. So, yeah, it's been pretty much every day since then.\n\nDoctor: When you start worrying, do you find it hard to stop or control the worry?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's really hard to stop. I try to just tell myself to calm down and focus on one thing, but it doesn't work. One worry just immediately leads right into another one. Before I know it, I'm checking my calendar, looking over my budget, and going through my assignments over and over again... and I end up not actually getting anything done. It's like I just can't turn it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel physically tense, like your muscles are tight or you can’t relax?\n\nPatient: Um, I haven't really noticed that, actually. I mean, I feel stressed out all the time, but I don't think my muscles get super tight or anything. I don't think so, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed كثيراً lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm stressed and overwhelmed with everything I have to do, but I don't feel depressed or sad. It's more just the constant worrying about getting it all done.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or keyed up?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel restless, exactly. Like, I'm not pacing around the room or anything. It's more just... my mind is always racing. I don't feel keyed up in my body, it's just my thoughts that won't stop.\n\nDoctor: Have these worries been interfering with how well you function in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes, they really have. I've actually started missing deadlines at work recently, which is... well, it's not like me. And I'm falling behind in my certificate classes, too. I just can't seem to keep up. I've even stopped doing all the normal routines that used to keep me grounded. Like, I haven't been doing yoga, or running... I don't even cook anymore. It just feels like everything is slipping.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is bigger or more out of proportion than the actual work and school problems in front of you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... to me, it doesn't feel out of proportion. The deadlines at work are real, and the classes actually are really demanding. It just feels like a massive amount of pressure that I have to deal with, so the worry feels pretty justified to me. It's just... a lot to handle.\n\nDoctor: Did your worrying begin after you started the graduate certificate program while working full-time?\n\nPatient: Yes, it did. It's been going on for... I'd say about a year and three months now. That's exactly when I started the evening graduate certificate program while trying to keep up with my full-time analyst job.\n\nDoctor: If you were not dealing with the graduate certificate program, do you think you would still be worrying this much?\n\nPatient: I don't know, honestly. I mean... maybe? The certificate program is definitely a huge part of the stress, but... I'm not sure if taking it away would completely stop the worrying. I feel like I might just find something else to stress about, you know? But it's hard to say for sure since I'm right in the middle of it all.\n\nDoctor: Aside from work and school, do you also find yourself worrying a lot about other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It feels like it spills over into everything. My mind just keeps jumping around from whether I’m falling behind at work, to... whether I’m just wasting money on school, to whether my family will think I can’t handle things. It's like I end up treating every small issue that comes up like it could completely derail my whole future.\n\nDoctor: Before you started the graduate certificate program, were you already someone who worried a lot about many different things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I think I just had normal stress before. If I had a big deadline at work, I'd be stressed about that, but... I was never someone who worried about a million different things all at once. It wasn't until I started the program that my mind started doing this.\n\nDoctor: If the graduate certificate program ended tomorrow, do you think your worrying would mostly go away?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, part of me wants to say yes, because it's such a huge source of stress right now. But since my worrying has kind of bled into everything else—like my job and my family—I'm not sure if it would just stop. I feel like my brain is so used to spinning out of control now that it might just latch onto something else. But I really can't say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your anxiety is mainly a reaction to the graduate certificate program?\n\nPatient: I mean, it definitely started when I began the program. That was the trigger, I guess. But now... I don't know if I can say it's just a reaction to that anymore. Like I said, it's kind of taken over everything else. So, it started with school, but now it's just... everywhere.\n\nDoctor: When you can’t sleep, is it usually because your mind is worrying?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's exactly it. Honestly, my sleep has basically fallen apart. I'll just... lie in bed in my apartment for hours. My laptop will be closed for the night, but my mind is still going. I'm just mentally rechecking work deadlines, thinking about tuition payments, going over class assignments... it just doesn't stop. And even when I finally do manage to sleep, I wake up feeling like my brain never really turned off.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

It's like I just can't turn it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel physically tense, like your muscles are tight or you can’t relax?\n\nPatient: Um, I haven't really noticed that, actually. I mean, I feel stressed out all the time, but I don't think my muscles get super tight or anything. I don't think so, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed كثيراً lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm stressed and overwhelmed with everything I have to do, but I don't feel depressed or sad. It's more just the constant worrying about getting it all done.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or keyed up?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel restless, exactly. Like, I'm not pacing around the room or anything. It's more just... my mind is always racing. I don't feel keyed up in my body, it's just my thoughts that won't stop.\n\nDoctor: Have these worries been interfering with how well you function in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes, they really have. I've actually started missing deadlines at work recently, which is... well, it's not like me. And I'm falling behind in my certificate classes, too. I just can't seem to keep up. I've even stopped doing all the normal routines that used to keep me grounded. Like, I haven't been doing yoga, or running... I don't even cook anymore. It just feels like everything is slipping.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is bigger or more out of proportion than the actual work and school problems in front of you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... to me, it doesn't feel out of proportion. The deadlines at work are real, and the classes actually are really demanding. It just feels like a massive amount of pressure that I have to deal with, so the worry feels pretty justified to me. It's just... a lot to handle.\n\nDoctor: Did your worrying begin after you started the graduate certificate program while working full-time?\n\nPatient: Yes, it did. It's been going on for... I'd say about a year and three months now. That's exactly when I started the evening graduate certificate program while trying to keep up with my full-time analyst job.\n\nDoctor: If you were not dealing with the graduate certificate program, do you think you would still be worrying this much?\n\nPatient: I don't know, honestly. I mean... maybe? The certificate program is definitely a huge part of the stress, but... I'm not sure if taking it away would completely stop the worrying. I feel like I might just find something else to stress about, you know? But it's hard to say for sure since I'm right in the middle of it all.\n\nDoctor: Aside from work and school, do you also find yourself worrying a lot about other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It feels like it spills over into everything. My mind just keeps jumping around from whether I’m falling behind at work, to... whether I’m just wasting money on school, to whether my family will think I can’t handle things. It's like I end up treating every small issue that comes up like it could completely derail my whole future.\n\nDoctor: Before you started the graduate certificate program, were you already someone who worried a lot about many different things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I think I just had normal stress before. If I had a big deadline at work, I'd be stressed about that, but... I was never someone who worried about a million different things all at once. It wasn't until I started the program that my mind started doing this.\n\nDoctor: If the graduate certificate program ended tomorrow, do you think your worrying would mostly go away?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, part of me wants to say yes, because it's such a huge source of stress right now. But since my worrying has kind of bled into everything else—like my job and my family—I'm not sure if it would just stop. I feel like my brain is so used to spinning out of control now that it might just latch onto something else. But I really can't say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your anxiety is mainly a reaction to the graduate certificate program?\n\nPatient: I mean, it definitely started when I began the program. That was the trigger, I guess. But now... I don't know if I can say it's just a reaction to that anymore. Like I said, it's kind of taken over everything else. So, it started with school, but now it's just... everywhere.\n\nDoctor: When you can’t sleep, is it usually because your mind is worrying?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's exactly it. Honestly, my sleep has basically fallen apart. I'll just... lie in bed in my apartment for hours. My laptop will be closed for the night, but my mind is still going. I'm just mentally rechecking work deadlines, thinking about tuition payments, going over class assignments... it just doesn't stop. And even when I finally do manage to sleep, I wake up feeling like my brain never really turned off.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any days during the past year when work and school were not pressing, but you still found yourself worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I can't think of a single day in the past year where work and school weren't pressing, to be honest. There's always some project going on at my job, or an assignment due for my classes. So... I haven't really had a day where those things were completely off my plate to even know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your level of worry is greater than the situation actually calls for?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Logically, I know it is. But my mind just keeps jumping around. I'll worry about whether I'm falling behind at work, and then it immediately shifts to whether I'm wasting money on school... or if my family is going to think I can't handle things. It's like I treat every small issue that comes up as if it could completely derail my whole future. So, yes, it's definitely more intense than what the situation actually calls for.\n\nDoctor: If your mind feels calm at bedtime, do you still have trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. It's been so long since my mind was actually calm at bedtime that I can't even remember. I just always have something I'm stressing about when I try to go to sleep.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I'm stressed and overwhelmed with everything I have to do, but I don't feel depressed or sad. It's more just the constant worrying about getting it all done.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or keyed up?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel restless, exactly. Like, I'm not pacing around the room or anything. It's more just... my mind is always racing. I don't feel keyed up in my body, it's just my thoughts that won't stop.\n\nDoctor: Have these worries been interfering with how well you function in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes, they really have. I've actually started missing deadlines at work recently, which is... well, it's not like me. And I'm falling behind in my certificate classes, too. I just can't seem to keep up. I've even stopped doing all the normal routines that used to keep me grounded. Like, I haven't been doing yoga, or running... I don't even cook anymore. It just feels like everything is slipping.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is bigger or more out of proportion than the actual work and school problems in front of you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... to me, it doesn't feel out of proportion. The deadlines at work are real, and the classes actually are really demanding. It just feels like a massive amount of pressure that I have to deal with, so the worry feels pretty justified to me. It's just... a lot to handle.\n\nDoctor: Did your worrying begin after you started the graduate certificate program while working full-time?\n\nPatient: Yes, it did. It's been going on for... I'd say about a year and three months now. That's exactly when I started the evening graduate certificate program while trying to keep up with my full-time analyst job.\n\nDoctor: If you were not dealing with the graduate certificate program, do you think you would still be worrying this much?\n\nPatient: I don't know, honestly. I mean... maybe? The certificate program is definitely a huge part of the stress, but... I'm not sure if taking it away would completely stop the worrying. I feel like I might just find something else to stress about, you know? But it's hard to say for sure since I'm right in the middle of it all.\n\nDoctor: Aside from work and school, do you also find yourself worrying a lot about other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It feels like it spills over into everything. My mind just keeps jumping around from whether I’m falling behind at work, to... whether I’m just wasting money on school, to whether my family will think I can’t handle things. It's like I end up treating every small issue that comes up like it could completely derail my whole future.\n\nDoctor: Before you started the graduate certificate program, were you already someone who worried a lot about many different things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I think I just had normal stress before. If I had a big deadline at work, I'd be stressed about that, but... I was never someone who worried about a million different things all at once. It wasn't until I started the program that my mind started doing this.\n\nDoctor: If the graduate certificate program ended tomorrow, do you think your worrying would mostly go away?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, part of me wants to say yes, because it's such a huge source of stress right now. But since my worrying has kind of bled into everything else—like my job and my family—I'm not sure if it would just stop. I feel like my brain is so used to spinning out of control now that it might just latch onto something else. But I really can't say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your anxiety is mainly a reaction to the graduate certificate program?\n\nPatient: I mean, it definitely started when I began the program. That was the trigger, I guess. But now... I don't know if I can say it's just a reaction to that anymore. Like I said, it's kind of taken over everything else. So, it started with school, but now it's just... everywhere.\n\nDoctor: When you can’t sleep, is it usually because your mind is worrying?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's exactly it. Honestly, my sleep has basically fallen apart. I'll just... lie in bed in my apartment for hours. My laptop will be closed for the night, but my mind is still going. I'm just mentally rechecking work deadlines, thinking about tuition payments, going over class assignments... it just doesn't stop. And even when I finally do manage to sleep, I wake up feeling like my brain never really turned off.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any days during the past year when work and school were not pressing, but you still found yourself worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I can't think of a single day in the past year where work and school weren't pressing, to be honest. There's always some project going on at my job, or an assignment due for my classes. So... I haven't really had a day where those things were completely off my plate to even know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your level of worry is greater than the situation actually calls for?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Logically, I know it is. But my mind just keeps jumping around. I'll worry about whether I'm falling behind at work, and then it immediately shifts to whether I'm wasting money on school... or if my family is going to think I can't handle things. It's like I treat every small issue that comes up as if it could completely derail my whole future. So, yes, it's definitely more intense than what the situation actually calls for.\n\nDoctor: If your mind feels calm at bedtime, do you still have trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. It's been so long since my mind was actually calm at bedtime that I can't even remember. I just always have something I'm stressing about when I try to go to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Are you still actively enrolled in the graduate certificate program right now?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I am. I'm still taking classes for it right now. That's, um, kind of what I meant earlier when I said I always have assignments due."
